ParlaMint-HR_2005-11-15-0.u46789	Dear President of the Parliament, distinguished MPs and MPs. In front of the Government of the Republic of Croatia and the Ministry of Finance, we propose the Law on Amendments and Supplements to the Foreign Affairs Act. The Foreign Exchange Act regulates the business of authorised exchangers by prescribing that exchange operations can be carried out by residents with legal personality status and by entrepreneurs of individuals using a protected computer program for carrying out exchange operations with a business bank and registered for the performance of such transactions. The adoption of this Act on Amendments and Supplements to the Foreign Exchange Act is foreseen as a legislative measure in the draft under the National Programme of the Republic of Croatia for the Association of the European Union for 2005. The draft law has been initiated by criticism and recommendations of the Council of Europe Special Committee for the assessment of measures against money laundering, which highlights the need for the regulation of procedures and the authorisation to carry out exchanges operations, respecting similarity criteria for exchangeholders. Those recommendations aim to extend the preventive system for the prevention of money laundering and to the activities of exchange offices, in accordance with Article 1 of the Directive of the European Parliament and of the Council. On the basis of the above, the proposal for a law amending and supplementing the Foreign Exchange Act was therefore drawn up, with the final proposal for a law which, in addition to the conditions laid down in the applicable law, introduces an obligation to obtain the approval of the Croatian National Bank for the conduct of exchange operations. For this purpose, the provisions of this law provide for documentation which applicants for the authorisation to carry out exchange operations shall apply to the request for reasons and for the refusal of applications as well as the reasons for the withdrawal of the authorisations granted. The activities of the issue, the withdrawal of the authorisation for the conduct of exchange operations, have been placed in the jurisdiction of the Croatian National Bank since, in its jurisdiction, the adoption of regulations governing the operation of authorised exchangers as well as the control of the operations of banks with which the authorised exchangers conclude a contract on the conduct of exchange operations. The Croatian National Bank also certified the computer programmes that the authorised exchangers are obliged to perform when performing the exchange operations. The transitional and final provisions of this Act provide that authorised exchangers operating under the applicable law shall comply with the provisions of this Act within one year of the date of entry into force of this Act. The second thematic area, which is amended and complemented by the provisions of this law, is the use of data that residents are obliged to deliver to the Croatian National Bank on foreign transactions and transactions. In view of the differences in understanding to which data must be used that the Croatian National Bank collects for the purpose of monitoring the projection of the balance of payments of the Republic of Croatia, this law adds to a provision that formalises the possibility that the collected data, except for statistical purposes, should also be used for the purposes of foreign exchange control. It shall also be added to a provision whereby the provision of data on the supervised person required in percentages of the surveillance is not considered to be an official secret. Those provisions are in line with the supervisory role of the Croatian National Bank and the foreign inspectorate of the Ministry of Finance. So much for the government, thank you.
ParlaMint-HR_2005-11-15-0.u46790	Thank you very much. The committee's rapporteurs. Legislative, finance and state budget, European integration? I'm opening a debate. HDZ representative club, respected representative Nevenka Majdenic. Here you go.
ParlaMint-HR_2005-11-15-0.u46791	Dear Mr. President of the Croatian Parliament, honourable representatives of the government, colleagues and colleagues. The Croatian Democratic Community MPs' club discussed the draft Law on Amendments and Supplements to the Foreign Affairs Act and fully supports it. We believe it is necessary to point out the particular basic reasons for proposing amendments and additions to this law, which can be summarised in three basic facts, which are firstly that the existing law came into force in June 2003 and birth in accordance with the provisions of the Stabilisation and Association Agreement with the European Community of the Republic of Croatia, under which it had to be able to liberalise certain segments of capital transactions in foreign exchange. The full liberalisation of foreign exchange must be achieved by the Republic of Croatia to achieve full membership in the European Union. Second, the Special Committee of the Council of Europe negatively assessed certain provisions of the applicable law and recommended that it be amended and supplemented. The evaluations of the Special Committee of the Council of Europe attach great importance to the Council of Europe in particular in the criteria of strengthening the system for the prevention of money laundering, the stability of financial institutions and ensuring confidence in the entire financial system. Third, the legislative regulations of this area are also provided for in the national programme of the Republic of Croatia for joining the European Union for 2005 in the chapter on freedom of movement of capital and in the chapter on the prevention of money laundering. Realising the position of the Republic of Croatia and the government's efforts in the process of joining the European Union, these reasons must be accepted. The law accurately regulates two important thematic areas and extends the conditions prescribed by the applicable law. The first thematic area covers the conditions under which a resident, a domestic legal person, can carry out exchange operations. An obligation to obtain a licence from the Croatian National Bank is introduced, the conditions to be met by a resident who wants to carry out the tasks of an authorised exchanger are laid down, the documentation is prescribed to prove that those conditions are met and the authorisation procedure is prescribed. We also consider a very important provision containing the reasons and conditions under which the approvals granted are withdrawn. In this law, it is prescribed that exchangeholders operating under the applicable law must comply within a year with the provisions of this law. If they do not comply with this obligation within the prescribed period of one year, they shall be obliged to apply for the removal from the court register where they are registered within 30 days. In addition, this law introduces a criterion of similarity for exchangeholders under which the Croatian National Bank does not grant approval if the owner of the craft or member of the management of a legal person is punishable for criminal offences prescribed by this law, i.e. for the criminal offence of money laundering. In addition, if the documentation attached is not possible to establish the identity of the applicant, whether it is the owner of a craft or a legal person or its share in the underlying capital, the authorisation shall not be granted. This means that very strict criteria have been introduced for exchangeholders, which we consider to be a significant improvement of the law. These conditions are precisely regulated in Articles 46a, 46b and 46c of the new law which precisely regulates what must contain a request for the authorisation to carry out exchange operations, what is the method of deciding on a request for the authorisation to carry out exchange operations and what are the reasons for the withdrawal of the authorisation to carry out exchange operations. In particular, it should be stressed by the provision which requires the authorised exchanger to carry out the tasks based on a pre-contract with the Croatian National Bank or a pre-contract with the manufacturer of the computer programme to deliver the contracts in question or to withdraw the authorisation. The second thematic area governed by this law is the area of use of data that residents are obliged to deliver to the Croatian National Bank, i.e. the Ministry of Finance. So far, there has been a dilemma or a difference in understanding to which this data may be used. This Law now adds a provision that allows the use of collected data, except for statistical purposes and which is very important, both for the control of foreign exchange and foreign foreign exchange transactions. This provision is in line with the supervisory function of the Croatian National Bank and the Foreign Affairs Inspectorate of the Ministry of Finance. Furthermore, by supplementing Article 49, residents are obliged to report to the Croatian National Bank on capital operations with non-residents and, at the request of the State Inspectorate, to provide information on the real owners of non-residents with which the business was concluded, especially if he is outside the territorial entrepreneur. The new provision added in Article 56 states that the provision of data in the supervised person required in the surveillance procedures is not considered to be an official secret. The Croatian National Bank will determine the conditions, i.e. the content, manner and procedure of reporting non-residents. The supervisory authorities involved in the supervisory and control procedure shall exchange information with each other, and a special act will regulate the way and content of the exchange between them. Penalties prescribed for legal and natural persons are appropriate because it has been proven that fines and prisons can introduce order and discipline in the implementation of any law, including this one. These provisions shall contain, in addition to fines and prison sentences and protection measures, the prohibition to carry out activities, and the withdrawal of foreign cash or cash in kunas in whole or in part. The prescribed criminal provisions may be harsh at first sight, but their consistent application will allow for equal conditions for the work of foreign exchange institutions. Strengthening these activities and liberalising capital transactions, which is ultimately the objective of this Act. This is ultimately our mission in the process of harmonising the legislation of the Republic of Croatia with the legal gains of the European Community. That is why, as I said at the outset, the Croatian Democratic Community's club supports the adoption of this Law by an urgent procedure, especially as we still have a lot to do if we want to keep pace until we reach full membership in the European Community. And that's all our undeniable and unique goal and commitment. Thank you.

ParlaMint-HR_2005-11-15-0.u46809	Mr. President of the Parliament, distinguished MPs and MPs! Therefore, the Government of the Republic of Croatia proposes the Law on the Croatian Financial Services Control Agency. So, in July 2002, the Croatian Parliament reached a conclusion on the need to unite non-bank sector supervisory institutions. Therefore, the Government of the Republic of Croatia decided at a session held on 27 October 2004 to launch a process for the unifying of non-bank financial sector supervisory institutions as of 2005. On 15 November 2004, the finance minister made a decision on the appointment of a task force to draft the law, and in the process of drafting the law, the unification of existing supervisory institutions is also part of consulting companies. The existing supervision institutions that are united in this law at the Croatian Financial Services Supervisory Agency are the following: the Securities Commission of the Republic of Croatia, the Pension Funds and Insurance Supervisory Agency and the Insurance Companies. The Agency shall be established as an independent independent legal person responsible for the Croatian Parliament. The main objectives of the Agency are to promote and maintain the stability of the financial system and to monitor the legality of the business of supervisory entities, in this case the non-banking financial institution. The agency has a management that consists, so here it is proposed in law, of five members of which is one president. The President and members of the Agency's administration shall appoint and resolve the Croatian Parliament on a proposal from the Government of the Republic of Croatia. The Council is the advisory body of the Agency, consisting of nine members of which three members are appointed by the Government of the Republic of Croatia and the other five members are elected by representatives of the associations of surveillance entities in the Croatian Chamber of Commerce while the chairman of the Agency's administration is a member of the board of directors of the Agency's office. The agency will oversee non-bank financial institutions whose assets today cross 40 billion kuna, increased for all emissions and turnovers of shares and bonds that occur on the Zagreb and Varazdin stock exchange. The current market capitalisation of both markets is about 150 billion kuna. The establishment of a single institution for supervision of non-bank sector financial institutions and a national risk management project and the development of the financial market. This is even more important because the European Union is introducing so-called this year. 42 open market measures and defines the PAN European network of Member States' supervisory institutions. When it comes to the benefits of united or consolidated supervision from other countries' experiences, then most often the following are pointed out. The combined supervision means greater professional capacity for supervision of financial conglomerates, enabling better flow of information and increasing the degree of coordination of the supervisory authority. Interinstitutional traffic within financial conglomerates are better monitored, monitoring standards are harmonised and regulatory means are increased. The competition between individual supervisory institutions and the organisation of functional surveillance is dodging. The possibility of early crisis detection and better crisis management and avoiding regulatory arbitration, therefore, the problem of real competence for a particular problem. The adoption of this Act will finance the agency in a mixed manner both from the state budget and from fees, non-financial banking institutions up to 0,8 prominences to be engaged from assets and revenues held by financial institutions or supervisory entities. The individual burden of fees will be separately governed by the rules on fees which will regulate the basis and frequency of the collection, the fees taking into account the specificity of each institution on the financial market in, under the supervision of this Agency. It is estimated that in the first years, the agency will finance up to 1/3 of the necessary expenditure, while the rest would be settled from the state budget, so that the total amount from the state budget in the first years would not exceed the amount of expenditure now spent from the budget for all three institutions that we have listed, therefore, commissions, agencies and directorates, which is around 25 million kuna. Therefore, this establishment of the Agency does not create additional budgetary expenditure. With the development of the market and the increase in the basis of the fee, the level of expenditure of the agency that is being settled from the state budget should also be reduced and the share financed by supervisory entities should be increased. This Law does not cover regulation and supervision of banks that continue to oversee the Croatian National Bank, one of the main supervisions for this is the complexity of the process, complete unification especially due to the introduction of a new "basel 2" standard in 2007 and any implications that could result from new regulatory supervision of banks. However, the provisions of this Act also lay down the obligation of the Agency and the Croatian National Bank to cooperate with which the prudential regulation and supervision sector of banks will be attached to the agency in the medium term. This Law shall enter into force on 8 days from its publication in the National Journal and shall apply from 1 January 2006.

Therefore, the decision is to unite only supervision of non-banking financial institutions while supervision of banks would remain in the Croatian National Bank's control and supervision of savings and credit associations in the Ministry of Finance's intelligence. On 15 November 2004, the finance minister made a decision on the appointment of a working group to draft a bill from officials of the Ministry of Finance and all supervisory institutions, but not on the proposal of those institutions. That's what I think we can logically conclude. Namely, the Government of the Republic of Croatia's decision of 27 October 2004 as before and said, it tasks the Ministry of Finance to submit by September 2005 a final proposal for a legal act to implement the process of uniting the supervisory institutions of the non-banking financial sector into one agency. It is not stated in the decision that this is also the deadline until which the Government of the Republic of Croatia intends to send to the parliamentary procedure, and if it is the date when the Government of the Republic of Croatia intended to send it to the parliamentary procedure, why the Ministry of Finance did not make a proposal before and delivered it to the Government of the Republic of Croatia in due time to debate and refer to further procedures. The second allegation is not true, because again, as was said in the July 2002 conclusion, there is another conclusion of the Croatian Parliament on the same subject since 12 June 2003, after being delivered to it a programme of unification, which reads: "A proposal for a programme of supervision of financial institutions and financial markets on a consolidated basis is accepted." And below 2. "The Government of the Republic of Croatia undertakes to establish a permanent committee to coordinate the regulation of financial services as soon as possible." We do not believe the promoter was familiar with this second conclusion of the Croatian Parliament. The absurdity of the adoption of this law by emergency procedure is even higher, as I said earlier, in the provisions of Article 159. The Rules of Procedure of the Croatian Parliament which reads: "Extreme law can only be passed by emergency procedure when the interests of defence and other particularly justified state reasons are required, that is, in order to prevent or eliminate major disturbances in the economy." What are particularly justified state reasons, i.e. what are possible or existing disorders that the Government of the Republic of Croatia is seeking to prevent or eliminate by including this law by means of an emergency procedure? All three supervisory institutions, as far as I know, whose unification is proposed to work normally. The report for the past year has been delivered to the Croatian Parliament or the Government of the Republic of Croatia and it has been accepted, the situation in the financial system is almost best under their supervision from now on, so what imposes on the urgent adoption of this law, we must ask ourselves, and we must eventually seek some answers. Regardless of the above, since the Government of the Republic of Croatia's proposed proposed a number of amendments, which will be addressed to the proposal for this law, are also high and low-level and half-determined. Furthermore, if it is already going into unification, the HSS wants it to be implemented in a quality way, i.e. that all organisational, technical, personnel and personnel preparations are carried out before the formal unification itself, and not simply from three institutions create three different sectors, three different locations, and then to enjoy walking who knows how long the problems of functioning are solved which will inevitably lead to the neglect of the basic functions of these agencies. The main difference between this proposal and the amendment of the HSS and the proposal for the law of the Government of Croatia is primarily in the direction of the new Commission's advice. While, in the proposal of the Government of the Republic, the Council of the Commission is merely an advisory body in the proposal of the HSS and its amendments, the Council has a governing role to play in determining the policies involved in the action of the Commission and the strategic decision-making process, while operational decisions would be taken by the Commission's administration. In our opinion, the advice should play a certain role or should not be. The provisions in the proposal of the Government of the Republic of Croatia as the advice of the Agency give an opinion of expert and scientific advice for the development of supervisory practice sound good, but what if no one asks or needs those opinions. It can happen that the advice can never theoretically and never meet. The difference in the proposal for a way of financing the commission under the Croatian government and HSS law is also different. The financing of a new supervisory institution from the State budget's resources from the property and income of supervisory entities and compensation for the services provided and the competence of the supervisory institution's part-round, however, exists and these are important. The Croatian Government's proposal provides for a compensation of up to 0.8% on the burden of supervisory entities, with the possibility that if the unpaid fee is not sufficient to cover the expenditure, the lack of funds is compensated from the state budget. In the event that the unpaid fee in the total revenues of the Agency exceeds the expenditure, the surplus shall be the revenue for the following calendar year. In other words, the burden of funding of the agency will easily fall on the burden of the participants. The HSS proposal goes from a ratio of at least 51% to a burden on the state budget and a maximum of 49% on the burden on control entities. As has been said in advance, the current commission and HAGENA are being financed entirely on the burden of the state budget, and only the directorate is being financed on the burden of surveillance entities. Since the expenditure of these three supervisory institutions in 2004 amounted to 25.3 million kuna, the expenditure of the Directorate amounted to 4.2 million kuna, i.e. only 16% on the burden of control entities. We are afraid that this approach would be a very devastating funding that would quickly fall on the burden we feel a state budget in full or in the most part. In this way, a quality balance would be established that would allow for all those relations resulting from such funding. What is also important, as already mentioned in this law, are activities in so-called “the law. ” a transitional period in order to avoid the destruction and operation of a new supervisory institution in a long time, neglecting its basic function and mission. Under the proposal of the Government of the Republic of Croatia, the Agency will adopt its statutes within 60 days of the date of application of the law and submit it to the Croatian Assembly for confirmation, and within 6 months of the date of entry into force of the Statute, acts listed by that law will be adopted. This practically means that almost all the following year, the agency and its employees will do their own work, not their own. Under the proposal for an amendment to be tabled by the HSS on the statutes, dealings with the statutes and organisation of internal acts would be dealt with by a small group of people, mostly members of the new administration, and everyone else would normally do their current work. So, according to us in the HSS, this law aims to achieve an effective process of uniting the content of three supervisory institutions into one supervisory institution in a transitional period of one year to two years, to do all the pre-productions that are necessary and which will not interfere with the implementation of its law of the prescribed tasks after the start of work. This is an HSS proposal that I think would be helpful in reorganising and changing supervision of financial services. Although there is no clear foothold in other countries' experiences about such a way of working, we in the HSS have nothing against uniform surveillance and roads in this direction, but we warn of all these dangers that I have put forward and that we will change our amendments, that is, to make the law more quality and not to produce those effects that no one wants. The fact is that the work of supervisory institutions at this point is quite satisfactory, that the situation on the financial market is relatively stable and there is no reason to rush through the adoption of this Law, so HSS would, of course, suggest that this goes to another reading. But since it's an emergency procedure, we in the HSS are going to make amendments in this sense that I was talking about. Thank you.

So, investment funds of 8 and a half billion kuna, formerly converted into holdings of 4 billion kuna, traffic on the Zagreb and Varazdin stock exchange of some 95 billion kuna, 4 compulsory pension funds with assets of 10 billion kuna, 24 insurance companies with assets of 14.4 billion kuna, 15 leasing companies with assets of 15 billion kuna, etc. All in all the people who will be in this agency, the people who will lead it should control the capital at the same time as the Croatian State budget. Of course, we wonder if it will really be effective, we wonder if this kind of unification will produce the right results. It is clear that the development of the economy also means the development of the financial sector. That, with the development of the financial sector, we must have far better institutions to monitor. But the big question is whether this model is really effective, whether it will be effective and what will happen to other legal acts that should improve the work of this agency. Such an institution should therefore have powers, strength and size almost as former financial police, so that it is indeed an effective institution. Better supervision will not be achieved by inflation of institutions rather than by the effectiveness of legal implementation. What is particularly important, however, is that, at the same time as we have the unification of the institution for the supervision of all these institutions, or economic operators dealing with financial services, on the other hand, a number of separate laws are being prepared, according to our information, to regulate private types of economic services, financial services. So, we will have separate laws for leasing, separate insurance laws and so on, while on the other hand we are going to a large system of uniting supervision of these entirely separate and different financial services. In this regard, we are interested in two very specific questions. Is it one thing for this Croatian Financial Services Supervisory Agency today, tomorrow to be responsible for monitoring micro-credit institutions and the other for the entire system of savings-credit cooperatives? We believe that savings-credit cooperatives as deposit institutions should be in the same group with banks and should be monitored together with banks by the Croatian National Bank. In all the countries to which we respect the structure of financial institutions is the following. We have a central, central bank, we have deposit institutions, so banks and deposit savings institutions, so savings banks, savings banks, savings-credit cooperatives, cooperatives, housing savings, postal savings, etc. And thirdly, we have non-depositary financial institutions, these are insurance companies, pension funds, investment funds,... houses, microcredit facilities and the like. [[The speaker doesn't understand.]] If this Agency refers to non-depositary institutions, then it is okay, but from such an explanation it is not clear whether the savings and credit unions are in the jurisdiction of this Agency, which in our court is bad or in the jurisdiction of the Croatian National Bank, which is good in our court. On the other hand, it is unclear where microcredit institutions are in all of this. Are they really here under this Agency, is she monitoring them, or will she be under some new body again? Without such clear answers, we cannot support this law. Thank you.

ParlaMint-HR_2005-11-15-0.u46837	Thank you very much, Mr. Vice President of the Croatian Parliament, colleagues and colleagues. As I said before, the current situation in the field of supervision of financial services is quite dysfunctional, too broad and at least complicated. There are 4 financial institutions that oversee financial services and markets, which are the Securities Commission, the Pension Fund Supervisory Agency and HAGENA, the insurance company supervisory directorate and the Croatian National Bank. In addition, the Ministry of Finance and, within the framework of the Financial System Administration, oversees the work of another 123 savings and credit cooperatives by the following year, when the Savings and Credit Cooperatives Act will be adopted. These financial institutions, in line with the growth and development of the financial services market and acted on different legal basiss of around 10 laws. In addition, these institutions are responsible for their work differently, some of the Parliament, some of the Government and some of the Parliament and the Government, which questioned the efficiency and coherence of the control of their work. By the new law of the first three mentioned agencies, therefore, the Securities Commission, the Pension Fund and Insurance Supervisory Agency, the Financial Services Supervisory Directorate, an independent, independent legal person responsible for the Croatian Assembly with fundamental objectives, which is: promoting and preserving the stability of the system and overseeing the legality of the supervision entities. The benefits of this united surveillance, which have already been seen in the countries where it is used, are significant, and I will only point out some. First, more professional human potentials unite. A better flow of information is enabled. Monitoring is standardised and efficiency is increased and the problem of competence is avoided. Another advantage is the creation of a single financial institution, which opens up the possibility of adopting a single Financial Services Act in due time. It will be a great relief in the application, monitoring and supervision and sanctioning of this matter because we now have about a dozen laws and even more accompanying regulations, regulations, regulations, decisions and the like. Furthermore, the implementation of this Act will not require additional budgetary resources, as the current way of financing will be taken over i.e. from the budget and the fees. So the same form and the same means from which the existing supervisory institutions were financed. The experience of countries where such joint supervision applies shows that the share of funding from the budget is reduced and that, by gradually strengthening and developing the financial market, the share of funding from supervisory entities is fully switched to financing. Finally, the unification of supervisory functions is primarily in the interest of institutions providing financial services and the development of the financial market and the interest of the Government that protects the stability of the financial system, I propose that this law be adopted in an emergency procedure. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-HR_2005-11-15-0.u46865	Thank you very much, Mr. Vice President. Dear MPs and MPs, the Ministry of Finance therefore proposes a final proposal to amend and supplement the Customs Law. I note, therefore, that all changes are and in a certain way these laws happen gradually. The government adapts to any change under customs laws within the European Union. That's the way it is in this case. Therefore, two essential features of the proposed amendments or additions to the Customs Code are. Continuing the process of harmonising Croatians with European customs procedures, and achieving a degree of efficiency to which customs services, the European Union countries. The proposed amendments and additions should also contribute to the simplification and acceleration of the implementation of customs procedures by applying European criteria in the assessment of the degree, the risk of certain forms of cross-border trade and the construction of a normative basis for establishing European standards of customs service handling in the implementation of customs procedures. The implementation of the European Standards for Risky Goods allows for further simplification of customs procedures for all participants in customs legal services who carry out their obligations in a timely and timely manner to the state budget and provide the necessary guarantees for the settlement of their customs obligations. The efficient implementation of customs procedures significantly narrows the area by the abitral behaviour of customs officers, and thus all forms of irregular implementation of procedures including the possibility of corruption. The implementation of European standards of transport procedures requires the completion of a Croatian guarantee system for the settlement of public debt in the transport procedure in particular with regard to its more efficient and cheaper implementation. In accordance with European standards, certain provisions on the creation and limitation of customs debt are specified. The proposed law regulates the provisions on customs offences in order to comply with their respective amendments or the provisions of customs regulations, rules on the protection of cultural goods or animals, etc., or compliance with the level of penalties prevented under general infringements. Taking into account the complexity of customs infringement procedures, it is proposed to extend the time limit for antiquity of customs offences from two to three years. Furthermore, a new modernised EU Customs Code will be adopted during 2006. Preparations are under way for the adoption of the Croatian Customs Code and its implementing rules to be adapted to the circumstances related to the imminent accession of the European Union. The content of the new Customs Code would fully comply with the new customs code, any other legal norms as a national part of customs law, would be transposed into customs regulations, or rules on customs and financial infringements. The government, since it is a law that has the P.Z.E. mark, therefore a mark for compliance with European legislation, proposes that this law be adopted by an urgent procedure. Thank you very much.

The HNS Club will therefore support this proposal for a law amending and supplementing the Customs Code. In addition to harmonising with EU legislation, the government has proposed amendments to and additions to the Customs Code and with the aim of creating preconditions for the smoothing of customs procedures and improving the legal certainty of participants in customs legal traffic. In particular, provisions on the demarcation of the customs office's establishment units which perform operationally in the implementation of export, import or transit customs procedures shall be specified. What is important to highlight in the amendments to the law was also taken into account the acceleration and cost-effectiveness of customs procedures as well as the full protection and interest of the state budget in such a way as to create mechanisms for avoiding, or preventing customs fraud, increasing the level of protection and equating customs service practices. These are issues which pay great attention to the countries of the European Union, and at the same time are issues that we must be very interested in in Croatia and which we must pay even more attention to, above all, because of ourselves, and not just to comply with EU legislation. In Croatian customs practice in recent years, several open issues have been recorded in the implementation of new Europeanised customs rules, both from the point of view of the legal certainty of the participants in the customs procedure and the protection of the interests of the state budget. The procedure for the adoption of new rules of the customs system is under way in the European Union, the modernised customs code which should enter into force next year, and in any case significantly prior to Croatia's accession to the European Union. In this case, Croatia will have to work even more to modernise and bring its customs legislation into line, as well as all customs services. We therefore propose to the Government of the Republic of Croatia very soon to send a proposal to the Croatian Parliament to amend the Customs Service Act, as it will thus show that indeed professional and quality public service and professional and quality employees.

ParlaMint-HR_2005-11-15-0.u46881	Thank you, Mr. Vice President, Mr. Representatives and MPs. The Ministry of Finance therefore proposes to the Parliament the State Support Act. Therefore, before the very reasoning of the State Aid Act, it is very important to point out the main features of this law and the reasons for its adoption. First of all, the proposed draft law represents one of the most important steps in joining the European Union, and as it relates directly to a competition whose screening process as we know it is ongoing and started last week. The adoption of this law, which is aligned with the acquis, is one of the most important conditions for achieving the so-called acquis. Bencmark, which will therefore be determined by the European Commission and by the opening of a chapter of competition in negotiations with the European Union. The possible implications of not adopting this law and its discrepancies can therefore lead to the postponement of the competition chapter, which is one of the most difficult chapters of negotiations between the candidate country and the European Union. What is very important to emphasise, therefore, most of us, therefore also MPs, are aware of the obligations of the Republic of Croatia on the Stabilisation and Association Agreement, and it is therefore necessary to specify the importance of this international agreement and its characteristics in the adoption of the law in question. In particular, following the conclusion of a Stabilisation and Association Agreement between Croatia and the European Communities and their Member States and the Interim Agreement on Trade and Related Issues between the Republic of Croatia and the European Community, and with regard to the establishment of a system of state support to the European Union's consistent state support system, which we seek to comply with by this law. So it found that the adoption of the aid law would be harmonised and adopted immediately after it was ratified, so the Stabilisation and Association Agreement, which de facto happened at the beginning of this year. The State aid system in the European Union is based on primary and secondary legal sources, as well as on the case law of the European Court of Justice. These legal sources are an integral part of aquis communitaire, i.e. the legal gains of the European Community, which the candidate countries are obliged to take over as a prerequisite for membership of the European Union in their national legislation and legal system. The implementation of the State aid system introduced in the Croatian legal system by adopting the State aid Act and the State aid regulation in 2003 has shown in practice the shortcomings related to the primary procedure of submitting and the proposals for granting state aid, as well as the lack of knowledge and experience related to this extremely complex and legal economic process. On a number of occasions, the European Commission warns the technical consultations that the Republic of Croatia does not respect its own laws, and points out on a number of occasions that it is also a violation of the signed Stabilisation and Association Agreement in certain areas. Continued practice is clearly indicated by the European Commission that the Republic of Croatia will lose credibility in this area, which can be extremely awkward and dangerous in the further process of negotiations between the Republic of Croatia and the European Union. The observations and suggestions made by the European Commission are therefore accepted by the Republic of Croatia because it is evident that we have a obligation to comply with the legal property of the Union as a consequence of the signed Stabilisation and Association Agreement of 2001, which this esteemed House has ratified. Therefore, taking into account the achievement of the strategic objective, preparations for the full membership of the Republic of Croatia in the European Union should be implemented through this law further harmonisation and preparation primarily in the implementation sense of the state support system with the EU State support system. Therefore, the purpose and purpose of this law is to prepare for the application of the State aid system after receiving the Republic of Croatia in full membership in accordance with the outcome of the negotiations. By receiving European Union membership, the law in question ceases to apply as Croatia takes over the law in force in the European Union. Since the scope of the necessary amendments and additions for further harmonisation with the acquis of the European Community is significant, it is proposed to adopt a new State Aid Act to be implemented next. Therefore, the direct integration of the State aid system governed by this law with the obligations of the Republic of Croatia referred to in Article 70. This appropriation is intended to cover expenditure relating to officials and temporary staff in the ‘Communication’ policy area of the ‘Communication’ policy area of the ‘Communication’ policy area. Stabilisation and Association Agreements between the Republic of Croatia and the European Communities. Amendment to the provisions of the old Articles 8 and 9 for the purpose of effective action by the Competition Protection Agency under the General Administrative Procedure Act, the Budget Act, with the exception of Article 266. The General Administrative Procedure Act on a proposal from the European Commission, the introduction of a more flexible setting of deadlines and delivery and the regulation of the question of repetitive requests for a reason of more effective treatment before the Competition Protection Agency, as the procedure which has been based on the distinction of general and individual state aid has proved ineffective. The most significant changes in relation to the current law relate to the enforcement procedure of the application, approval, control and recovery of unlawful State aid granted. In this respect, compliance with the acquis which is incorporated into the law in a way that introduces a more efficient process of delivery, approval, control of the implementation and recovery of state aid to the Competition Protection Agency. So, in the law, there are approximately three cases. The positive parties are therefore that such a law and such a draft law aimed at harmonising the Croatian economy with the acquis, above all through the establishment of an effective system of implementation and control of state aid, is intended to ensure a fair competition for all market participants. This means that this law seeks to ensure that none of the market participants is in an unequal position by favouring the granting of state support. For example, the granting of support under the selectivity system or, for example, even discrimination against state aid users puts a more favourable position than a non-state support economic operator, which has a ultimate impact on the deterioration of competition on the market and on the impact of the result of business entities. On the other hand, the recipient of State aid conditionally stated in the largest number of cases through the acquisition of state aid covers its inefficientness which may be significantly weaker than other competition on the market. This approach is contrary to the rules of the competition where, at the very least, taxpayers' money is used to cover up the lack of business of a particular economic operator and to favour competition in a market that does not receive the same aid. The studies have shown that the model of market discrimination through a selective approach to conferring benefits on certain economic operators has in extreme terms resulted in higher costs of the planned benefits by providing State support. The positive effects therefore, by addressing these extremely significant issues of the Croatian national economy, will create more favourable conditions for allocating state aid in the most effective way in making sure that all market players have equal treatment and as set out in this law and the regulation that will be completed after the adoption of this law. This applies, for example, to the investment and provision of state aid in research and development, small and medium-sized enterprises, etc. On this road, the proposed State Aid Act is one of the first steps towards achieving full membership of the Republic of Croatia in the European Union of negotiations relating to the competition chapter. Because of the importance of this law, we propose that the law be passed in an emergency procedure. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-HR_2005-11-15-0.u46896	Thank you very much, Mr. President of the Croatian Parliament, respected members of the Croatian Government, distinguished colleagues and colleagues. Before us is the Law, we have before us a proposal for a Law on State Support with the Final Bill proposed by the Government of the Republic of Croatia to adopt the proposed Law by an urgent procedure, because we have seen several clubs that have asked for a second reading. This legislative proposal aligns the legislation of the Republic of Croatia with the legislation of the European Union. I would like to point out that the current State Aid Act was passed in 2003, and amendments and additions were made in 2004. The State aid law is in line with the new organisation of the Competition Protection Agency, in accordance with the Competition Protection Act, which corrects nomotechnical errors in the text and is in line with the statement of Article 5. This appropriation is intended to cover expenditure relating to the provision of additional appropriations in accordance with Article 21(3) of the Financial Regulation. Stabilisation and Association Agreements between the Republic of Croatia and the European Community and their Member States. The adoption of the State aid law and the State aid regulation, the implementation of the State aid system in practice showed shortcomings in the procedure for submitting the State aid proposal. But there are also shortcomings in knowledge and experience related to the legal and economic system. As the aim of the Republic of Croatia is full membership in the European Union, further coordination and preparation of the state support system of the Republic of Croatia with the EU's state support system is needed to achieve this strategic objective. In doing so, we must make sure that further harmonisation does not prejudge future negotiations on the accession of the European Union within this area. Taking into account the fact that, by entering full membership, the State Aid Act and all other domestic legal regulations are put outside the scope of the State Aid Act. At the same time, the legal acquis of the European Union has been applied directly to which Member States have transferred competences in the area of State aid. The purpose and purpose of this regulation is to prepare the Republic of Croatia after receiving full membership of the European Union for the application of the EU State Support System. As the scope of the necessary changes and additions for further harmonisation with the acquis of the European Community is significant, the best solution is to adopt a new State Aid Act. The new State aid laws shall be implemented as follows. This Decision shall enter into force on the twentieth day following that of its publication in the Official Journal of the European Union. Stabilisation and Association Agreements. Nomotechnical change in the concept of "appropriation" favoured by the conceptual adjustment of the acquis of the European Community. Further harmonisation of the definition of general prohibition, exemptions from a general prohibition with the acquis of the European Community, as proposed by the European Commission. The Commission shall adopt delegated acts in accordance with Article 21(3) of Regulation (EU) No 1306/2013. Stabilisation and Association Agreements between the European Community and their Member States and the Republic of Croatia, as proposed by the European Commission. For the purpose of effective action by the Competition Protection Agency in accordance with the General Administrative Procedure Act and the Budget Act, with the exception of Article 266 and amending the provisions of Articles 8 and 9 on a proposal from the European Union. The introduction of a more flexible setting of delivery and regulation deadlines, the question of repetitive requests for a more effective procedure before the Competition Protection Agency. The current process of delivery, approval, supervision, implementation and recovery of state aid to the Competition Protection Agency was based on the distinction between general and individual state aids, it proved very ineffective, and this new Law proposes to introduce a more effective procedure on the previously stated. This proposal distinguishes two possibilities. The first option is if the State aid proposal is contained in the legislative proposal. The Competition Protection Agency shall issue a mandatory preliminary opinion to the Government of the Republic of Croatia and the Croatian Parliament on this legislative proposal. Another option is if the proposal for state support is in the process before the Government of the Republic of Croatia or the proposal for State support is in the process before other state and other bodies. At that time, the Competition Protection Agency shall participate in the procedure by granting a mandatory prior authorisation. It is important to point out that the intolerance of the proposal for acts containing provisions on the granting of State aid to the approval, i.e. the adoption of acts containing State aid provisions without the approval of the Competition Protection Agency, leads to illegality. It is therefore proposed that the Competition Protection Agency in this case order the repayment of funds if they are used by the basis of such non-existent provisions. The new Law also proposes compliance with the provisions on the so-called, appropriate measures from the legal property of the European Community. Nomotechnical harmonisation of the provisions on official secrecy in accordance with the European Commission's proposals. A new deadline is also proposed for submitting an annual report as proposed by the European Commission in order to comply with the deadlines of the European Commission. The Club of Representatives of the Croatian Democratic Community notes Article 10 of this Proposal for a Law because it considers it an unacceptable solution to which the opinion of the Competition Protection Agency is binding. Because in this way, the Croatian Parliament cannot pass the law unless it has the Agency's opinion, or if it is not positive. This shall also apply in an appropriate manner to sub-legislative acts, i.e. acts of the body of units of local and regional self-government relating to Articles 11 and 12. They would also make an observation by the HDZ Club on the transitional and final provisions of Articles 21 to 25 where it is necessary to work legally and no-technically on their statements. The Croatian Democratic Community's representative club supports the urgent procedure because this Law continues to bring Croatia's state support system into line with the EU's state support system, and the basis of this law also requires the adoption of sublegal acts that are detailed in the system. Finally, I would like to say that the Croatian Government has a designed development strategy to implement this new proposal of the State Support Act, which will be supported by the Croatian Democratic Community Representatives' Club. Thank you.

The Stabilisation and Association Agreement between the Republic of Croatia and the European Union, Article 70(4), defines that Croatia will establish an operational body, means an independent operational body which will be entrusted by Croatia with the powers of approval of the State support programme, i.e. individual support, and orders the return of State support if these support is unlawfully granted. The clear objective and intention was to prevent or disrupt competition in trade between the European Community and Croatia, and to prevent possible abuses of the ruling position of one or more entrepreneurs in the territory of the Community, or the Republic of Croatia, and to prevent and disrupt competition by creating a monopoly. As the state aid system so far has proved to be a lack of this bill, it gradually matures awareness of the need to correct the perceived shortcomings clearly in the current state aid system. Fortunately for us, and fortunately for this whole project, the entire course of this transformation of the state support system will take place as part of harmonising our legislation with the legislation and legal gains of the European Union in the process of negotiating Croatia's accession to the European Union. It means that this is one in a series of our preparedness tests to accept the European Union standards because the State aid system of the Republic of Croatia needs to be harmonised in implementing terms with the European Union's state support system. What our support system is is really well known to all of us. If we are to be honest, we will see that the method of adoption was burdened with various advantages and was under strong pressure from politics and various informal often breeding and volunteer groups. The effectiveness of the support is often questionable from the views put above all economic and political objectives. So far, we have had one proverb as a privilege, meaning the notion of privilege itself, instead of which this law introduces the notion of advantage and in order to give an advantage to someone must be indicated and defined transparent criteria that must be met by the support user. What is most important here in this legislative proposal is that this law clearly prescribes the role of the Competition Protection Agency. This shall comply with the obligations laid down in the Stabilisation and Association Agreement. This agency is an independent operational body expected to be effective in the supply, efficiency in approval, effectiveness in monitoring the implementation and efficiency in the return of State aid if they prove to have occurred illegally. It means that the Competition Protection Agency approves and oversees the implementation of state aid all in order to ensure transparency of the State aid system. The key role of the Agency is to consider and assess state aid proposals, monitor and implement and the effects of State aid granted, in the collection and processing of and the recording of State aid data. Its role is to submit a report to the Croatian Parliament on these aids themselves and to participate in the preparation of laws and regulations to improve the state support system. The agency's not an obstacle, she's a helper. Here it has been indicated in previous discussions that it is an obstacle to some parliament or government to make appropriate decisions autonomously. We see it, our club sees the agency as an expert-legislative filter between act promoters, so there are ministries, local administrations, self-government, or other competent authorities for applying for support and means on the one hand, and the Government and the Parliament on the other hand, so that both the Government and the Parliament can be helped not to fall into error, that it is not in a position to break the law or rules of the profession. Article 10 provides for an earlier positive binding opinion by the Agency. If there is no positive binding opinion, there is no State support according to this. Many will be distracted by this formulation in this article and other articles in which this body becomes a truly independent body. It seems to me that in one of these formulations there is room for further manipulation and that what is wanted with this legislative proposal is not realized. Stabilisation and Association Agreements. So it is also this law today, with all of it at the very least, to be decent to the unusual provisions, is actually the consequence of the Stabilisation and Association Agreement, so let me quote the key parts of Article 70 to simply remove some doubts and to avoid any further discussions. In accordance with Article 70(3) of the Treaty on the Functioning of the European Union, any State aid which distorts or threatens to distort competition is incompatible with the application of the Agreement by favouring certain undertakings or certain products. Article 4 states that Croatia will establish an operational independent body to which it will entrust the powers necessary for the full application of this Article within one year from the date of entry into force of this Agreement. This authority shall be empowered to approve State support and individual support programmes in accordance with paragraph 2 of this Article. Furthermore, paragraph 7 states that for the first 4 years, any state aid granted by Croatia will be assessed taking into account that Croatia is considered to be an area of the community described in Article 87 of the Treaty establishing the Community and that within 3 years of the entry into force of the SAA, Croatia will identify to the Commission its GDP amounts per capita, and that this body will provide the Commission with a map of support, develop a regional support map, and the Commission of the European Communities will assess the similarity of the Croatian regions on the basis of appropriate Community guidelines. Thus, it is clearly said here that Croatia will establish: a) an independent body, therefore, of the Government an independent body, so a body that will in fact be only competent and only responsible for the level of support. Moreover, it is clear from this that the Government is obliged to submit reports to that body and to request permission for a level of support. What Parliament? What Parliament? The agency is the only one that matters. That Agency, or that independent body, shall coordinate its support level with the requests of the Commission of the European Communities. So with the interests and economic needs of existing members of the European Union. Not with the interests and needs of the Croatian economy or Croatian regions, but with the interests and needs of the European Union based on relevant Community guidelines. End of story. So the Parliament in all of this actually has almost no role to play. The agency's reporting to us for the previous year, and that's it. Are we going to accept that report or not? That's completely irrelevant. Because the Agency actually approves of the ministries of what form of support they can give at all. This is based on the European Commission's approval, i.e. on the basis of a regional support map approved by the European Commission. So much for the role of the Croatian Parliament in determining state support and so much for the importance of such a Stabilisation and Association Agreement. Of course, it was celebrated at all the bells of the people who negotiated it, but when a man sees what it says, he wonders if that speed was actually paid with such concessions. That this House is making decisions on state aid and that these decisions are implemented by the Government of the Republic of Croatia or that the Government gives its decisions to this High House for adoption. That we actually have an entirely parallel mechanism through an agency that is in charge of support, which does not correspond to the Parliament but corresponds to the European Commission. But it's ladies and gentlemen the consequence of this Stabilisation and Association Agreement. And now we only have two options, right? It is one thing to ask for an amendment to this Agreement, which is, of course, difficult to expect through the negotiation system to resolve these parts so that we no longer have to rely on the SAA, but it is quite clear that as long as Croatia becomes a member of the European Union, the SAA is the only international legal framework for our communication with the European Union. And the second option is to make aid law so that, despite this article, it leaves enough room for both the government and the Parliament to use their constitutional powers. Everything else is, in fact, ladies and gentlemen, going beyond the framework of the Croatian Government and giving this agency the Croatian Constitution. Because she is also the body of the executive authority, and the executive power corresponds to the Croatian Parliament, not the European Commission. So for those reasons, as long as the law is written like this, we can't support it. Thank you.

ParlaMint-HR_2005-11-15-0.u46940	Thank you very much, Mr. Vice President, ladies and gentlemen of the Parliament. Allow me in short lines to explain this proposal. In order to prevent and reduce the grey economy, the Government proposed last year at the 8th month, and the Croatian Parliament adopted the Law on special conditions for placing flour on the market. In this context, they can say that this Law has delivered its results on the elimination of the grey economy in the area of flour and the results of the application of the Law on specific conditions for the placing of flour on the market for the period from 1 August to 31 December 2004 are evident. The production and transport of flour is subject to control of the system of monitoring the issued, used high-accuracy records and the consumption of wheat and the quantities of flour obtained, and thus the total turnover of the same. For the first time in the Republic of Croatia, a register of mills, bakers, manufacturers of intended mixtures and concentrates is established, as well as importers of flour, intended mixtures and concentrates. During 2004, it was registered in the register of 167 millers, 11 producers of intended mixtures and concentrates, 18 importers of flour, 22 importers of intended mixtures and concentrates, a thousand 419 bakers, and we estimate that the bakery has significantly more than the number entered in the Ministry's register. Of the 167 millers registered, 40 of them did not apply for stamps. Based on the inspection of the State Inspectorate, most of them temporarily or permanently stopped working. In the remaining 127 millers, they were increased by cca 20% in 2004 compared to the same period in 2003. Unfortunately, we can conclude that by introducing flour stamps to the greatest extent, we have eliminated the grey market for flour, but it has been observed that in the field of bakery there is a great presence in the gray economy. And the inspectors of the State Inspectorate are unable to act properly. The use of flour in the manufacture of bakery products or better said, how many out of 100 kilograms of flour can be produced on average kilograms of bread and pastry is not legally regulated. So there is a possibility of avoiding payment of commitments to the state. When we look at both statistical data and data on grinding, then we come to the conclusion that almost 2/3 of the total consumption of bakery products is still a grey economy. For this reason, it is proposed to amend and supplement the Law on specific conditions for placing flour on the market and to make a special regulation laying down the quantities of flour used in the manufacture of certain bakery products. Therefore, by amending and supplementing the Law, the following things will be regulated. The amendment and addition of Article 6 of the existing Act enables the adoption of a new Regulation which will prescribe the maximum quantities of flour used for the production of certain bakery products and how to keep records. By amending and supplementing Articles 7, 8, 9, 10 and 11. The law on special conditions for the placing of flour on the market provides for criminal provisions for legal and natural persons, infringers in bakeries. The new rule, which can be said to be in the final stage of production, will detail the standards for the consumption of flour for all types of bakery products, including all types of bread, pastry, and other bakery products, and thus the competent inspectors will have the legal basis for sanctioning the offender. And therefore, here, by applying the law and by adopting a new regulation to the greatest extent, the grey market will be contained in both flour and baker's products, and we estimate that a significant part of the money will be poured into the state budget. And therefore, I also thank you once again for accepting the application of the emergency procedure. Thank you very much.

The very fact that the Law on Courts is in force, the Law on Courts passed in late '93. So far changed nine times speaks of its importance for the judiciary, but then for the overall exercise of the right of citizens to access the court. I would not say that he is talking about some wandering rather than the need for the law to be better, to be even more improved and to be in line with the needs of citizens, and on the other hand with European standards. The new Law on Courts now regulates what is normally governed by the Law on Courts, as a roof law, which is a structure, a circle, the actual jurisdiction of courts, the internal structure of courts, the internal structure of the Supreme Court, the rights and duties of judges, the conditions for the appointment and resolution of judges, and other important issues such as the issue of judicial administration. We finally distinguish between regular and specialized courts by opening up the possibility that, eventually, if it turns out, some new courts will form. However, what is important to say about this law in relation to the current law, which we hope to put beyond the scope of this law, is the expansion of citizens' rights to exercise the right to trial within a reasonable period of time. Because what are the citizens asking us to do? To have the right to a trial within a reasonable period of time. Unfortunately, the fact is she neither has a need to hide, nor can she hide, this is all the analysis, statistics that court proceedings take too long. Therefore, in accordance with other constitutional law, the right of citizens to be tried within a reasonable period of time they have the right to seek the protection of that right, in our case before the Constitutional Court before they go to the European Court of Human Rights. Because of the situation I have already cited that a large number of cases and their duration objectively puts many citizens in a position to address that court, there is a danger of the regular functioning of the Constitutional Court. The example is done, and you probably saw it in the February report of the Constitutional Court, this year, the number of constitutional lawsuits filed on this basis, so because citizens believe that the procedure takes too long has been increased from 2000 to 2004 by 14 times or 18% of the proceedings and cases handled by the Constitutional Court refer to this basis, therefore to the too long duration of the court proceedings. In order to enable the Constitutional Court to function regularly, on the one hand, in order to prevent the Constitutional Court from reacting to such a case, and, on the other hand, to enable citizens to exercise the right and on this basis, to enable them to resolve their request within a reasonable period of time, it is proposed that citizens may submit such a request directly to a higher court in the area of jurisdiction of the municipal court where they consider that the proceedings are too long. In the case of a county court as a second-degree court, the citizen goes directly to the Supreme Court and only in case of a complaint, i.e. the length of proceedings before the Supreme Court or that he is not satisfied with the decision of the lower court would he seek the protection of his right before the Constitutional Court. It is also important that our goal and one segment of the judiciary which will gain more and more importance is to bring judicial practice into line and to recognise the case law as someone who also creates the right, or practice, or the achievement of citizens when it comes to the application of certain rules. We are witnessing the fact that in certain cases citizens are deprived of the right to this constitutional principle, i.e. the uniform application of the law and the unification of judicial practice for reasons which say that in certain cases, because of their otherwise natural nature, a citizen cannot go to the Supreme Court on the original legal basis. Furthermore, what is an important segment does not directly affect the trial and decision-making itself, but it is important for the functioning of the court and the judiciary as a whole. ParlaMint-HR_2005-11-15-0.u46970	Mr. Vice President, Madam Secretary of State, colleagues and colleagues. Here is the Committee for the Justice of the Croatian Parliament at the 31st session held on 4 October 2005 on the Final Proposal for a Law on Courts tabled by the Government of the Republic of Croatia by the Act of 15 September 2005. The Committee considered the basis of Article 73. Rules of Procedure of the Croatian Parliament, draft law in the capacity of the home working body. In the opening statement, the applicant's representative briefly referred to the differences contained in the final proposal, resulting from the adoption of the proposal and the application of the bill. I am pleased to conclude, on behalf of the Committee, that the applicant has accepted all the objections made by the Justice Committee in the draft law in the first reading, except for the proposal of the Committee concerning the existing provisions governing the immunity of judges, which was not adopted by the Proposer because the Office for the Law of the Government of the Republic of Croatia gave a negative opinion. In the debate, the committee members supported the final draft law with the following comments and proposals. It is proposed to review the provision of Article 8 as it narrows the immunity of judges in relation to the existing provision of Article 9. Court law. Since the Constitution of the Republic of Croatia contains in Article 121 a provision that reads: "The Judges have immunity under the law", this gives the possibility and actually an obligation to develop the scope of immunity precisely by this law. The position on the question of the immunity of judges also took over the general session of the Supreme Court of the Republic of Croatia and delivered it to the Committee and the Proposer. The opinion of the Committee is that the provision of Article 26 is unclear, and the reasoning is wrong and that the words "judgmental administrations" should be deleted from it, as the supervision of the judicial administration is regulated in Article 62. In the direction of improving the said provision, the Committee suggests that Article 26 should be amended in such a way that the same provision reads: "The President of the High Court may, in order to assist in overseeing the conduct of the proceedings of the judge, appoint two or more judges. The provisions of Article 37 shall stipulate, inter alia, that the Judicial Council shall give an assessment of the conduct of its duties. The opinion is that when assessing the conduct of the judicial duties, the Judicial Council should consult the Presidents of the Courts in particular when assessing judges of municipal courts. In the light of Article 63, the words "judgments" should be deleted as judicial inspectors carry out judicial investigations in the courts. In Article 74(4) and Article 6 it is necessary to align the conditions of appointment of university professors of legal science for judges of regular and specialised courts. Finally, the disagreement of Article 109(3) with the provisions of Article 112(1) is suggested in a way that the same provision reads: "The President of the Court shall appoint a minister of justice among the candidates for which the Judicial Council has established that they meet the conditions for the President of the Court. The President of the Proposer, pointing out the proposals and objections to the provisions on the final draft law, pointed out that the proposals would be carefully considered and the proposals on Article 8 and Article 26 of the amendments to the applicants. After the debate was held, the Committee on Justice decided, with seven votes in favour, one vote against and one abstention, to propose to the Croatian Parliament to pass the Law on Courts. Thank you.
