ParlaMint-HR_2005-11-15-0.u46976	Mr. President, the Honorable Madam Secretary of State, colleagues and colleagues! The Croatian Democratic Community's representative club supports the Final Proposal for a Law on Courts. In fact, the uncontested justification for the adoption of the Law on Courts does not come only from the fact that the applicable law was passed on 30 December 1993 and has since been amended and supplemented several times already, and because of the obvious need of a new, therefore different normative arrangement and a new approach to the exercise of judicial duties on the one hand and cases of judicial administration on the other. The maturity of this regulation has just been contained in those provisions of the laws which guarantee and actually elaborate the constitutional principle contained in Article 117. The Constitution of the Republic of Croatia, by ensuring the independence and independence of the judicial authority, is precisely in such a way that no one or anyone has the right to interfere with the decision of the judges or in any way by pressuring the decision-making process. However, it should be pointed out that this regulation discusses the relationship of responsibility for the effective exercise of judicial duty seen through the citizens' imperative to exercise their rights to judicial protection within a reasonable period of time. It is precisely this newspaper that is absolutely a positive shift and one of the essential elements of the Croatian judiciary reform should be highlighted and related to concrete legal provisions. The proposed Article 26, i.e. beyond the proposed Article 26, is also added to the whole of the Head, so the regulation under the heading "Protect the right to trial within a reasonable period" and the articles 27 and 28 are detailed in this Institute. This is not just about constitutional protection of human rights and fundamental freedoms or personal and political freedoms and rights contained in the constitutional provision of Article 29. Therefore, each person has the right to decide, within a reasonable period, on his rights and obligations, either on suspicion or on charges of criminal offence, by means of a law established by an independent and impartial court, because the definition of infringement of the right to trial within a reasonable period of time, indicates that, in essence, this concept hides undue delay in the conduct of proceedings, as well as the adoption of concrete judicial decisions. The violation of this right comes as a result of the activities or actions of obvious courts, and it was precisely the courts that were regularly or specialised as the institutions most responsible for the exercise and protection of that right. The proposed legislative solutions regulate the powers to decide on the protection of the right and the trial within a reasonable period in such a way that even the role of the Supreme Court of the Republic of Croatia and the Constitutional Court of the Republic of Croatia are regulated in a specific way and legally justified manner by the nature, characteristics and jurisdiction and the Supreme Court of the Republic of Croatia and the Constitutional Court of the Republic of Croatia in a truly quality way when it comes to the development of this institute. Therefore, an application for the protection of injured rights shall be submitted directly to a higher court or tribunal which is objecting to undue delay in deciding whether or not it is regular or specialised courts and the possibility of applying to the Supreme Court. This Regulation shall enter into force on the twentieth The constitutional law of the Constitutional Court of the Republic of Croatia, therefore the institution of filing constitutional action as a means of protection exists after the previous legal path used, noting that Article 27(3) is just mentioned, the urgency of the action in terms of the submission of the claims in question, regardless of what court protection of that right is sought. However, here, as mentioned above, there should be no concern that the constitutional principle of independence and independence of the judicial authority should be highlighted as follows. First, if, on request, it is established that the court before which the proceedings under investigation have been unduly delayed by the conduct of the proceedings and the decision is authorised to set a time limit within which that court must decide in the dispute. Secondly, a lower court order applies only to the deadline. So, I repeat, only on time but not in a way of deciding in a concrete dispute. Third, if the court adopts a claim for the protection of the right to trial within a reasonable period of time or if it finds that the applicant has been injured by the right to trial within a reasonable period of time, it will determine the applicant and the appropriate compensation for infringement of the right to trial within a reasonable period of time, which was already sufficient in the previous discussions. Fourthly, in the appeal procedure, the Supreme Court of the Republic of Croatia decides and the applicant would not have the right to appeal only when the Supreme Court of the Republic of Croatia decides, upon request as a first-degree court. However, against the Supreme Court decision, a dissatisfied party has the possibility to protect its potentially injured right within 30 days of delivery of the decision by filing a constitutional suit to the Constitutional Court of the Republic of Croatia. Furthermore, in this context, the provision of Article 10(2) of the proposed law or the possibility that the President of the Supreme Court of the Republic of Croatia may, with the prior opinion of the General Meeting of the Court of Justice, determine that in a particular type of case, another really competent court is acting if a court which is really and locally competent because of a large number of cases which are in operation cannot discuss these cases within a reasonable period of time and decide. So it's essentially the same thing that was worked out with two really quality legal institutes. One refers to a reasonable trial and the other also continues to take place at the institute but as the possibility of adjudicating cases to those courts that are less burdened with the cases themselves. What I really want to highlight as an important thing in the Law on Courts in this proposed law is, therefore, the supervision of the judicial administration or the introduction of a judicial inspection by judges at work at the Ministry of Justice, which is the basis of Article 61, has the authority of the highest judicial authority on the one hand but also the possibility of overseeing the judicial administration by senior courts clearly constitute normative solutions based on which the effectiveness of the conduct of Croatian courts will be increased. Reduce the time needed to decide in specific cases, reduce the number of cases in court resolution, and ultimately ensure better functioning of the judiciary as a whole. From these last sentences, either the last sentences just come out or are listed all of those really problems that the Croatian judiciary is currently burdened with and are also explained by the institutes, i.e. which are translated into the Law on Courts and therefore the way in which specific problems in terms of the functioning of the judiciary and the basis of this Law and legal solutions will therefore be resolved. This classification or classification of courts from legal, logical and factual sides does not need to be explained because it is indeed in the classification of courts according to activity itself, jurisdiction best corresponds to the picture or in the way that the courts themselves should be classified in the Republic of Croatia. ParlaMint-HR_2005-11-15-0.u47004	Mr. President, State Secretary, colleagues and colleagues. First of all, I will say that I support this final proposal for a law on courts that lies ahead of us in a second reading. This new Law on Courts is certainly another step forward in the overall reform of the judiciary that will contribute to the greater quality and efficiency of the work of the courts, in general establishing the judiciary as a fair, legal, impartial and democratic system that must be independent without the possibility of political influence as well as legislative and enforcement authorities. Although that was the case during the first reading, I will repeat and highlight some of the newspapers proposed by this Law that I hold to contribute to this new quality of court work. One of these newspapers is the provision of Article 15, which allows the establishment of permanent services or departments outside the seat of a criminal, municipal or commercial court, and the possibility of the President of a directly higher court, the establishment of days of judgment, outside the court's seat, which will certainly contribute to the efficiency of the courts. A very significant newspaper is the provisions of Article 59 which regulate that when courts of the second degree on the same fact and legal matter are made different decisions and no exceptional legal remedy is allowed, it shall allow the parties within one year from the date on which the decision on which the disputed legislative provision was first applied to the Supreme Court to examine whether such a decision has compromised the uniform application of the law and the equality of citizens. I also consider that the applicant has complied with the observations and elaborated more precisely the principle of the trial within a reasonable period referred to in Article 4 and more precisely as set out in Chapter 3 of the protection of the right to trial within the reasonable time limits of Articles 27 and 28 of this proposal. I also support the introduction of judicial inspection, the extension of the powers of judicial advisers as well as the more precise regulation of objective criteria for assessing the work of judges as contained in Council of Europe Recommendation 9412. The law provides for the introduction of a property card for judges is also a newspaper that contributes to the credibility of judges, as it will strengthen the overall trust in justice system justice, independence, independence and impartiality of judges, and at least remove or at least reduce suspicion of possible corruption. Determining the property of judges as well as other civil servants is certainly one of the effective means of verifying the legality and regularity of their conduct. I will point out once again the need for unambiguous regulation of the selection procedure of the President of the Court for reasons as it seems to me that some contradictions remain in this final draft of the law between Article 109(3) and Article 112(1) which, if they remain in the proposed form, could lead to different interpretations and irregularities in the selection of the President of the Courts. In this regard, Article 109(3) regulates the appointment of the President of the Court, and the same is taken from the current law on the courts of Article 73a and reads: "The President of the Court shall appoint a minister of justice amongst the candidates proposed by the Judicial Council." While Article 112(1) determines that the Judicial Council shall determine candidates who meet the conditions for the President of the Court and shall propose that one of them be appointed as the President of the Court. In my first reading of this Law, I also set a concrete example when, in my opinion, it is because of the abuse of interpretation of the provision in force in Article 73. The Law on Courts, which regulates the election of the President of the Court of Justice, illegally appointed President of the Municipal Court in Vrbovac, because the former Minister of Justice, contrary to the unanimous proposal of the Judicial Council, appointed a candidate not only did not propose, but it expressed itself negatively on the competence of the Court. In order to avoid this kind of thing happening in the future, it is necessary to regulate precisely and unequivocally the selection procedure of the President of the Court, and to warn the Committee on Justice in its report of this disagreement between those provisions. For those reasons, I propose that the selection procedure of the President of the Court be as precise as possible in order for the applicant to comply with the provisions of Article 112(1) with Article 109(3) or to adopt a proposal for a formulation of the Committee on Justice. Thank you.
